267→\n   268→        # the two cameras see the tongue at different pixel scales\n   269→        self.side_scale = self._view_scale(self.side_tongue)\n   270→        self.bottom_scale = self._view_scale(self.bottom_tongue)\n   271→\n   272→    def _trial_velocity(self, feature):\n   273→        \"\"\"Frame resolution velocity of one tracked feature, one entry per trial.\"\"\"\n   274→        return {trial: self._velocity(self._track(trial, feature)) for trial in self.trials}\n   275→\n   276→    def _video_offset(self):\n   277→        \"\"\"Seconds by which the video clock leads the behavior clock (findVideoOffset.m).\"\"\"\n   278→        sample_rate = float(np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['fs'])[0])\n   279→        video_start = np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['bitcode']['bitstart']) / sample_rate\n   280→        behavior_start = trial_column(self.obj['bp'], 'ev', 'bitStart')\n   281→        return pd.Series(video_start).mode().iloc[0] - pd.Series(behavior_start).mode().iloc[0]\n   282→\n   283→    def _traj(self, trial, view):\n   284→        \"\"\"Tracking of one trial and camera, however the file stores it.\"\"\"\n   285→        traj = self.obj['traj'][view]\n   286→        if isinstance(traj, dict):                     # v7.3: one entry per camera\n   287→            return {field: traj[field][trial] for field in traj}\n   288→        entry = traj[trial]                            # v5: one entry per trial\n   289→        if hasattr(entry, '_fieldnames'):              # scipy leaves nested structs as objects\n   290→            return {field: getattr(entry, field) for field in entry._fieldnames}\n   291→        return entry\n   292→\n   293→    # camera frame time after correcting for the video offset and aligning to the go cue, in seconds\n   294→    def _frame_time(self, trial, view):\n   295→        \"\"\"Frame times of one trial and camera, in seconds from go cue onset.\"\"\"\n   296→        frame_times = np.ravel(self._traj(trial, view)['frameTimes'])\n   297→        return frame_times - self.offset - self.go_cue[trial]\n   298→\n   299→    def _camera_of(self, trial, feature):\n   300→        \"\"\"The camera that tracks a feature, and its tracking for this trial.\"\"\"\n   301→        for view in (SIDE, BOTTOM):\n   302→            traj = self._traj(trial, view)\n   303→            if feature in list(traj['featNames']):     # the name picks out the camera\n   304→                return view, traj\n   305→        raise KeyError('%s is tracked by neither camera' % feature)\n   306→\n   307→    # x, y, and likelihood within the time window\n   308→    def _track(self, trial, feature):\n   309→        \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature over the frames inside the window.\"\"\"\n   310→        view, traj = self._camera_of(trial, feature)\n   311→        x, y, likelihood = _feature(traj, list(traj['featNames']).index(feature))\n   312→        time = self._frame_time(trial, view)           # frame counts can differ between views\n   313→        inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n   314→        return time[inside], x[inside], y[inside], likelihood[inside]\n   315→\n   316→    # smooth the x, y postion, and compute the speed, filter by the likelihood threshold > 0.90\n   317→    def _velocity(self, track):\n   318→        \"\"\"Combined velocity over the valid frames, NaN elsewhere.\"\"\"\n   319→        time, x, y, likelihood = track\n   320→        speed = np.full(time.size, np.nan)\n   321→        if time.size < 2:                              # a few trials have no frame times\n   322→            return time, speed\n   323→\n   324→        sigma = SMOOTH_SIGMA_MS / 1000 / np.median(np.diff(time))\n   325→        valid = likelihood > LIKELIHOOD_CUT            # x and y are NaN below this\n   326→\n   327→        # smooth and differentiate inside each run of tracked frames, never across a gap\n   328→        for start, stop in _runs(valid):\n   329→            if stop - start < 2:                       # a lone frame has no velocity\n   330→                continue\n   331→            sx = gaussian_filter1d(x[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n   332→            sy = gaussian_filter1d(y[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n   333→            speed[start:stop] = np.hypot(np.gradient(sx, time[start:stop]),\n   334→                                         np.gradient(sy, time[start:stop]))\n   335→        return time, speed\n   336→\n   337→    def _view_scale(self, velocity):\n   338→        \"\"\"The percentile that puts one camera's velocity on a comparable scale.\"\"\"\n   339→        pooled = np.concatenate([velocity[trial][1] for trial in self.trials])\n   340→        return np.nanpercentile(pooled, NORMALIZE_PCT)\n   341→\n   342→    # the tongue is seen by both cameras, on different pixel scales, so each view is\n   343→    # binned and divided by its own percentile before the two are averaged\n   344→    def tongue_velocity(self):\n   345→        \"\"\"Binned tongue velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   346→        side = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.side_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   347→        bottom = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.bottom_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   348→        side = side / self.side_scale\n   349→        bottom = bottom / self.bottom_scale\n   350→        return _mean_over_available(side, bottom)      # one view alone where the other is missing\n   351→\n   352→    # only one paw stays tracked through the delay, so it is used on its own, in pixels\n   353→    def paw_velocity(self):\n   354→        \"\"\"Binned paw velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   355→        return np.array([_bin_frames(*self.paw[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n   356→\n   357→    # motion energy is already one number per frame, so it only needs binning\n   358→    def motion_energy(self):\n   359→        \"\"\"Binned motion energy, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   360→        rows = []\n   361→        for trial in self.trials:\n   362→            time = self._frame_time(trial, SIDE)       # motion energy follows the side camera\n   363→            inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n   364→            rows.append(_bin_frames(time[inside], self.energy[trial][inside]))\n   365→        return np.array(rows)\n   366→\n   367→    def outputs(self):\n   368→        \"\"\"The three streams, each cut in two at its own session median.\"\"\"\n   369→        tongue = self.tongue_velocity()\n   370→        paw = self.paw_velocity()\n   371→        energy = self.motion_energy()\n   372→        return (_discretize(tongue, np.nanpercentile(tongue, SPLIT_PCT)),\n   373→                _discretize(paw, np.nanpercentile(paw, SPLIT_PCT)),\n   374→                _discretize(energy, np.nanpercentile(energy, SPLIT_PCT)))\n   375→\n   376→\n   377→# ------------------------------------------------------------------- neural --\n   378→# Spike times are already on the behavior clock and relative to trial start, so aligning\n   379→# to the go cue is one subtraction. Quality is a free text label written with either case\n   380→# and with a few typos, so it is matched in lower case.\n   381→\n   382→def _quality(cluster):\n   383→    \"\"\"Quality label of a cluster; a handful carry no label at all.\"\"\"\n   384→    label = cluster['quality']\n   385→    return label.strip().lower() if isinstance(label, str) else ''\n   386→\n   387→\n   388→class Neural:\n   389→    \"\"\"The QC-passing units of a session, as smoothed firing rates.\"\"\"\n   390→\n   391→    def __init__(self, obj, probes, trials):\n   392→        self.obj = obj\n   393→        self.go_cue = trial_column(obj['bp'], 'ev', 'goCue')\n   394→        self.clusters = [cluster for probe in probes                # probes concatenated\n   395→                         for cluster in self._probe_clusters(probe)\n   396→                         if _quality(cluster) not in QUALITY_DROP]\n   397→\n   398→        # two sessions keep running after the recording stops, leaving trials with no spikes\n   399→        last = max(int(np.asarray(cluster['trial'], int).max(initial=0))\n   400→                   for cluster in self.clusters)\n   401→        self.trials = np.asarray([t for t in trials if t < last])   # trial numbers are 1 based\n   402→\n   403→    def _probe_clusters(self, probe):\n   404→        \"\"\"One dict per cluster on the given probe, however the file stores them.\"\"\"\n   405→        entry = self.obj['clu'][probe - 1]\n   406→        if isinstance(entry, dict) and isinstance(entry['tm'], list):\n   407→            # v7.3: one entry per probe, each field holding one array per cluster\n   408→            return [{field: entry[field][i] for field in entry}\n   409→                    for i in range(len(entry['tm']))]\n   410→        return self.obj['clu']                     # v5: one entry per cluster, single probe\n   411→\n   412→    def spike_count(self, cluster):\n   413→        \"\"\"Spikes of one cluster per trial and bin, aligned to the go cue.\"\"\"\n   414→        spike_trial = np.asarray(cluster['trial'], int) - 1         # trial numbers are 1 based\n   415→        spike_time = np.asarray(cluster['trialtm'], float) - self.go_cue[spike_trial]\n   416→\n   417→        # count spikes into a trial by bin grid; those outside the window fall off the edges\n   418→        trial_edges = np.arange(self.go_cue.size + 1) - 0.5\n   419→        counts, _, _ = np.histogram2d(spike_trial, spike_time, bins=[trial_edges, BIN_EDGES])\n   420→        return counts[self.trials]                                  # only the trials we keep\n   421→\n   422→    def rates(self):\n   423→        \"\"\"Firing rate of every unit above MIN_RATE, as (n_units, n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n   424→        sigma = RATE_SD_MS / 1000 / BIN\n   425→        rates = []\n   426→        for cluster in self.clusters:\n   427→            rate = self.spike_count(cluster) / BIN                 # (n_trials, 1, N_BINS)\n   457→\n   458→    return {\n   459→        'session_id': name,\n   460→        'subject': name.split('_')[0],\n   461→        'neural': [np.ascontiguousarray(rates[:, t, :]) for t in range(n_trials)],\n   462→        'input': [inp[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n   463→        'output': [out[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n   464→        'n_trials': n_trials,\n   465→        'n_units': int(rates.shape[0]),\n   466→    }\n   467→\n   468→\n   469→def assemble(results):\n   470→    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n   471→    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n   472→    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n   473→\n   474→    return {\n   475→        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n   476→        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n   477→        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n   478→\n   479→        'subjects': subjects,\n   480→        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n   481→\n   482→        'brain_regions': [BRAIN_REGION],\n   483→        'brain_region_idx': [np.zeros(r['n_units'], np.int32) for r in results],\n   484→\n   485→        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n   486→        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n   487→        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n   488→\n   489→        'metadata': {\n   490→            'task_description':\n   491→                'Head-fixed mice lick left or right for water. In the delayed-response '\n   492→                'context a tone instructs the direction, a delay follows, and an auditory '\n   493→                'go cue opens the response window; in the water-cued context all cues are '\n   494→                'omitted and water arrives at a random port. struct\n        return {k: _h5(f, node[k]) for k in node}\n    if node.attrs.get('MATLAB_class', b'').decode() == 'char':\n        return ''.join(chr(c) for c in np.asarray(node[()]).ravel())\n    if node.dtype == object:                                # Early-lick and photostim trials\n# are dropped, following the paper. The index keeps the original trial numbers, which\n# every later step uses to reach back into the raw arrays.\n\ndef trial_column(bp, *names):\n    \"\"\"One entry per trial from a field of bp, e.g. trial_column(bp, 'stim', 'enable').\"\"\"\n    n_trials = int(np.ravel(bp['Ntrials'])[0])\n    field = bp\n    for name in names:\n        field = field[name]\n    return np.ravel(np.asarray(field, float))[:n_trials]\n\n\ndef trial_info(bp):\n    \"\"\"Outcome, context, and lick direction per trial, without early-lick or photostim trials.\"\"\"\n    hit = trial_column(bp, 'hit') > 0\n    miss = trial_column(bp, 'miss') > 0\n    right = trial_column(bp, 'R') > 0\n    autowater = trial_column(bp, 'autowater') > 0\n    early = trial_column(bp, 'early') > 0\n    stim = trial_column(bp, 'stim', 'enable') > 0\n    n_trials = hit.size\n\n    # a hit is a correct lick and a miss an incorrect one; the animal ignored the rest\n    outcome = np.full(n_trials, OUTCOME['ignore'])\n    outcome[hit] = OUTCOME['correct']\n    outcome[miss] = OUTCOME['incorrect']\n\n    # water is given from a random port without any cue in the WC context\n    context = np.where(autowater, CONTEXT['WC'], CONTEXT['DR'])\n\n    # a hit licks the instructed port, a miss licks the other one, an ignore neither\n    lick_direction = np.full(n_trials, LICK['no lick'])\n    lick_direction[hit] = np.where(right[hit], LICK['right'], LICK['left'])\n    lick_direction[miss] = np.where(right[miss], LICK['left'], LICK['right'])\n\n    info = pd.DataFrame({'lick_direction': lick_direction, 'context': context,\n                         'outcome': outcome})\n    info.index.name = 'trial'\n    return info[~early & ~stim]\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------- camera --\n# Tongue velocity, paw velocity, and motion energy for one session. A class because the\n# video offset, the per-view scales, and the median split are session-wide\n# quantities that every trial reuses.\n\ndef _feature(traj, index):\n    \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature, whichever way the file orders ts.\"\"\"\n    ts = np.asarray(traj['ts'])\n    if ts.shape[0] == len(traj['featNames']):      # v7.3 reads it as (features, xyl, frames)\n        return ts[index]\n    return ts[:, :, index].T                       # v5 keeps MATLAB's (frames, xyl, features)\n\n\ndef _runs(valid):\n    \"\"\"First and last+1 index of each contiguous run of valid frames.\"\"\"\n    edges = np.diff(np.concatenate([[0], valid.astype(int), [0]]))\n    return zip(np.flatnonzero(edges == 1), np.flatnonzero(edges == -1))\n\n\ndef _bin_frames(time, values):\n    \"\"\"Mean of the valid frames in each bin, NaN where a bin holds no valid frame.\"\"\"\n    bin_of_frame = np.searchsorted(BIN_EDGES, time, side='right') - 1\n    valid = np.isfinite(values) & (bin_of_frame >= 0) & (bin_of_frame < N_BINS)\n    total = np.bincount(bin_of_frame[valid], weights=values[valid], minlength=N_BINS)\n    count = np.bincount(bin_of_frame[valid], minlength=N_BINS)\n    return np.where(count > 0, total / np.maximum(count, 1), np.nan)\n\n\ndef _mean_over_available(first, second):\n    \"\"\"Mean of the two, whichever one is there alone, or NaN where neither is.\"\"\"\n    stacked = np.stack([first, second])\n    count = np.sum(np.isfinite(stacked), axis=0)\n    total = np.nansum(stacked, axis=0)\n    return np.where(count > 0, total / np.maximum(count, 1), np.nan)\n\n\ndef _discretize(values, threshold):\n    \"\"\"Two classes split at the threshold; untracked bins take the trailing class.\"\"\"\n    code = np.where(values >= threshold, MOVEMENT['above threshold'], MOVEMENT['below threshold'])\n    return np.where(np.isnan(values), MOVEMENT['not visible'], code)\n\n\nclass Camera:\n    \"\"\"The three camera streams of a session, binned and cut into classes.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, obj, name, trials, datadir):\n        self.obj = obj\n        self.trials = list(trials)\n        self.go_cue = trial_column(obj['bp'], 'ev', 'goCue')\n        self.offset = self._video_offset()             # one constant for the whole session\n        self.energy = load_motion_energy(name, datadir)\n\n        # frame resolution velocity, for the two tongue views and for the paw\n        self.side_tongue = self._trial_velocity(SIDE_TONGUE)\n        self.bottom_tongue = self._trial_velocity(BOTTOM_TONGUE)\n        self.paw = self._trial_velocity(PAW)\n\n        # the two cameras see the tongue at different pixel scales\n        self.side_scale = self._view_scale(self.side_tongue)\n        self.bottom_scale = self._view_scale(self.bottom_tongue)\n\n    def _trial_velocity(self, feature):\n        \"\"\"Frame resolution velocity of one tracked feature, one entry per trial.\"\"\"\n        return {trial: self._velocity(self._track(trial, feature)) for trial in self.trials}\n\n    def _video_offset(self):\n        \"\"\"Seconds by which the video clock leads the behavior clock (findVideoOffset.m).\"\"\"\n        sample_rate = float(np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['fs'])[0])\n        video_start = np.ravel(self.obj['sglx']['bitcode']['bitstart']) / sample_rate\n        behavior_start = trial_column(self.obj['bp'], 'ev', 'bitStart')\n        return pd.Series(video_start).mode().iloc[0] - pd.Series(behavior_start).mode().iloc[0]\n\n    def _traj(self, trial, view):\n        \"\"\"Tracking of one trial and camera, however the file stores it.\"\"\"\n        traj = self.obj['traj'][view]\n        if isinstance(traj, dict):                     # v7.3: one entry per camera\n            return {field: traj[field][trial] for field in traj}\n        entry = traj[trial]                            # v5: one entry per trial\n        if hasattr(entry, '_fieldnames'):              # scipy leaves nested structs as objects\n            return {field: getattr(entry, field) for field in entry._fieldnames}\n        return entry\n\n    # camera frame time after correcting for the video offset and aligning to the go cue, in seconds\n    def _frame_time(self, trial, view):\n        \"\"\"Frame times of one trial and camera, in seconds from go cue onset.\"\"\"\n        frame_times = np.ravel(self._traj(trial, view)['frameTimes'])\n        return frame_times - self.offset - self.go_cue[trial]\n\n    def _camera_of(self, trial, feature):\n        \"\"\"The camera that tracks a feature, and its tracking for this trial.\"\"\"\n        for view in (SIDE, BOTTOM):\n            traj = self._traj(trial, view)\n            if feature in list(traj['featNames']):     # the name picks out the camera\n                return view, traj\n        raise KeyError('%s is tracked by neither camera' % feature)\n\n    # x, y, and likelihood within the time window\n    def _track(self, trial, feature):\n        \"\"\"x, y, and likelihood of one feature over the frames inside the window.\"\"\"\n        view, traj = self._camera_of(trial, feature)\n        x, y, likelihood = _feature(traj, list(traj['featNames']).index(feature))\n        time = self._frame_time(trial, view)           # frame counts can differ between views\n        inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n        return time[inside], x[inside], y[inside], likelihood[inside]\n\n    # smooth the x, y postion, and compute the speed, filter by the likelihood threshold > 0.90\n    def _velocity(self, track):\n        \"\"\"Combined velocity over the valid frames, NaN elsewhere.\"\"\"\n        time, x, y, likelihood = track\n        speed = np.full(time.size, np.nan)\n        if time.size < 2:                              # a few trials have no frame times\n            return time, speed\n\n        sigma = SMOOTH_SIGMA_MS / 1000 / np.median(np.diff(time))\n        valid = likelihood > LIKELIHOOD_CUT            # x and y are NaN below this\n\n        # smooth and differentiate inside each run of tracked frames, never across a gap\n        for start, stop in _runs(valid):\n            if stop - start < 2:                       # a lone frame has no velocity\n                continue\n            sx = gaussian_filter1d(x[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n            sy = gaussian_filter1d(y[start:stop], sigma, mode='mirror')\n            speed[start:stop] = np.hypot(np.gradient(sx, time[start:stop]),\n                                         np.gradient(sy, time[start:stop]))\n        return time, speed\n\n    def _view_scale(self, velocity):\n        \"\"\"The percentile that puts one camera's velocity on a comparable scale.\"\"\"\n        pooled = np.concatenate([velocity[trial][1] for trial in self.trials])\n        return np.nanpercentile(pooled, NORMALIZE_PCT)\n\n    # the tongue is seen by both cameras, on different pixel scales, so each view is\n    # binned and divided by its own percentile before the two are averaged\n    def tongue_velocity(self):\n        \"\"\"Binned tongue velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n        side = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.side_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n        bottom = np.array([_bin_frames(*self.bottom_tongue[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n        side = side / self.side_scale\n        bottom = bottom / self.bottom_scale\n        return _mean_over_available(side, bottom)      # one view alone where the other is missing\n\n    # only one paw stays tracked through the delay, so it is used on its own, in pixels\n    def paw_velocity(self):\n        \"\"\"Binned paw velocity, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n        return np.array([_bin_frames(*self.paw[trial]) for trial in self.trials])\n\n    # motion energy is already one number per frame, so it only needs binning\n    def motion_energy(self):\n        \"\"\"Binned motion energy, as (n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n        rows = []\n        for trial in self.trials:\n            time = self._frame_time(trial, SIDE)       # motion energy follows the side camera\n            inside = (time >= T_START) & (time <= T_STOP)\n            rows.append(_bin_frames(time[inside], self.energy[trial][inside]))\n        return np.array(rows)\n\n    def outputs(self):\n        \"\"\"The three streams, each cut in two at its own session median.\"\"\"\n        tongue = self.tongue_velocity()\n        paw = self.paw_velocity()\n        energy = self.motion_energy()\n        return (_discretize(tongue, np.nanpercentile(tongue, SPLIT_PCT)),\n                _discretize(paw, np.nanpercentile(paw, SPLIT_PCT)),\n                _discretize(energy, np.nanpercentile(energy, SPLIT_PCT)))\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------- neural --\n# Spike times are already on the behavior clock and relative to trial start, so aligning\n# to the go cue is one subtraction. Quality is a free text label written with either case\n# and with a few typos, so it is matched in lower case.\n\ndef _quality(cluster):\n    \"\"\"Quality label of a cluster; a handful carry no label at all.\"\"\"\n    label = cluster['quality']\n    return label.strip().lower() if isinstance(label, str) else ''\n\n\nclass Neural:\n    \"\"\"The QC-passing units of a session, as smoothed firing rates.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, obj, probes, trials):\n        self.obj = obj\n        self.go_cue = trial_column(obj['bp'], 'ev', 'goCue')\n        self.clusters = [cluster for probe in probes                # probes concatenated\n                         for cluster in self._probe_clusters(probe)\n                         if _quality(cluster) not in QUALITY_DROP]\n\n        # two sessions keep running after the recording stops, leaving trials with no spikes\n        last = max(int(np.asarray(cluster['trial'], int).max(initial=0))\n                   for cluster in self.clusters)\n        self.trials = np.asarray([t for t in trials if t < last])   # trial numbers are 1 based\n\n    def _probe_clusters(self, probe):\n        \"\"\"One dict per cluster on the given probe, however the file stores them.\"\"\"\n        entry = self.obj['clu'][probe - 1]\n        if isinstance(entry, dict) and isinstance(entry['tm'], list):\n            # v7.3: one entry per probe, each field holding one array per cluster\n            return [{field: entry[field][i] for field in entry}\n                    for i in range(len(entry['tm']))]\n        return self.obj['clu']                     # v5: one entry per cluster, single probe\n\n    def spike_count(self, cluster):\n        \"\"\"Spikes of one cluster per trial and bin, aligned to the go cue.\"\"\"\n        spike_trial = np.asarray(cluster['trial'], int) - 1         # trial numbers are 1 based\n        spike_time = np.asarray(cluster['trialtm'], float) - self.go_cue[spike_trial]\n\n        # count spikes into a trial by bin grid; those outside the window fall off the edges\n        trial_edges = np.arange(self.go_cue.size + 1) - 0.5\n        counts, _, _ = np.histogram2d(spike_trial, spike_time, bins=[trial_edges, BIN_EDGES])\n        return counts[self.trials]                                  # only the trials we keep\n\n    def rates(self):\n        \"\"\"Firing rate of every unit above MIN_RATE, as (n_units, n_trials, N_BINS).\"\"\"\n        sigma = RATE_SD_MS / 1000 / BIN\n        rates = []\n        for cluster in self.clusters:\n            rate = self.spike_count(cluster) / BIN                 # counts -> Hz\n            if rate.mean() > MIN_RATE:\n                rates.append(gaussian_filter1d(rate, sigma, axis=1, mode='reflect'))\n        return np.asarray(rates, np.float32)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------ session --\n\ndef process_session(name, datadir):\n    \"\"\"Convert one session into per-trial neural, input, and output arrays.\"\"\"\n    obj = load_mat(session_file(name, 'data_structure', datadir))\n    info = trial_info(obj['bp'])\n\n    neural = Neural(obj, SESSIONS[name], info.index)\n    info = info.loc[neural.trials]                 # drops any trial past the recording\n    n_trials = len(info)\n\n    rates = neural.rates()\n    tongue, paw, energy = Camera(obj, name, info.index, datadir).outputs()\n\n    # the three per-trial values are repeated across bins so all six outputs share one array\n    out = np.empty((n_trials, len(OUTPUT_NAMES), N_BINS), np.int8)\n    out[:, 0, :] = info['lick_direction'].to_numpy()[:, None]\n    out[:, 1, :] = info['context'].to_numpy()[:, None]\n    out[:, 2, :] = info['outcome'].to_numpy()[:, None]\n    out[:, 3, :] = tongue\n    out[:, 4, :] = paw\n    out[:, 5, :] = energy\n\n    inp = np.tile(TIME.astype(np.float32), (n_trials, 1, 1))       # (n_trials, 1, N_BINS)\n\n    return {\n        'session_id': name,\n        'subject': name.split('_')[0],\n        'neural': [np.ascontiguousarray(rates[:, t, :]) for t in range(n_trials)],\n        'input': [inp[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n        'output': [out[t] for t in range(n_trials)],\n        'n_trials': n_trials,\n        'n_units': int(rates.shape[0]),\n    }\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n\n        'brain_regions': [BRAIN_REGION],\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.zeros(r['n_units'], np.int32) for r in results],\n\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description':\n                'Head-fixed mice lick left or right for water. In the delayed-response '\n                'context a tone instructs the direction, a delay follows, and an auditory '\n                'go cue opens the response window; in the water-cued context all cues are '\n                'omitted and water arrives at a random port. Decoded variables: lick '\n                'direction, behavioral context, outcome, and tongue, paw, and whole-body '\n                'movement.',\n            'time_bin_size': BIN * 1000.0,          # ms\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'go cue onset (auditory chirp, 10 ms)',\n            'off_start': T_START,\n            'off_end': T_STOP,\n\n            'dataset': 'Hasnain, Birnbaum et al., Nat Neurosci 2024, ALM recordings',\n            'neural_units': 'Hz',\n            'session_selection':\n                \"The 44 sessions listed in the author's DataLoadingScripts/Recording and \"\n                'video/load<ANM>_ALMVideo.m that are present in data/, with the probe each '\n                'entry specifies; two-probe sessions are concatenated.',\n            'unit_curation':\n                'Cluster quality labels, lower cased, excluding %s, then units whose mean '\n                'rate over the window is at or below %.1f Hz.'\n                % (', '.join(sorted(QUALITY_DROP)), MIN_RATE),\n            'trial_curation': 'Early-lick and photostimulation trials excluded.',\n            'neural_processing':\n                'Spikes counted in %.0f ms bins, converted to Hz, smoothed with a gaussian '\n                'of %d ms standard deviation.' % (BIN * 1000, RATE_SD_MS),\n            'movement_discretisation':\n                'Tongue and paw velocity are the speed of the DeepLabCut position after a '\n                '%d ms gaussian, computed within each run of frames whose likelihood '\n                'exceeds %.1f. The two tongue views are each divided by their %dth '\n                'percentile before being averaged. Each stream is averaged into %.0f ms '\n                'bins and split at its own session %dth percentile; bins the camera did '\n                'not track take the trailing \"not visible\" class.'\n                % (SMOOTH_SIGMA_MS, LIKELIHOOD_CUT, NORMALIZE_PCT, BIN * 1000, SPLIT_PCT),\n            'session_info': [\n                {'session_id': r['session_id'], 'subject': r['subject'],\n                 'n_trials': r['n_trials'], 'n_units': r['n_units']}\n                for r in results\n            ],\n        },\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile', help='output pickle path')\n    ap.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', help='process all sessions (default)')\n    ap.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='process only 2 sessions')\n    ap.add_argument('--datadir', default=DATA_DIR,\n                    help='directory holding the Ephys_Behavior/ folders (default: %(default)s)')\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    names = SAMPLE if args.sample else list(SESSIONS)\n    print('%d sessions' % len(names), flush=True)\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    results = []\n    for i, name in enumerate(names, 1):\n        t1 = time.time()\n        res = process_session(name, args.datadir)\n        results.append(res)\n        elapsed = time.time() - t0\n        print('[%2d/%d] %-18s %4d trials %4d units  %5.1fs  (elapsed %5.1fs, total ~%5.1fs)'\n              % (i, len(names), name, res['n_trials'], res['n_units'],\n                 time.time() - 497→def good_quality(label: str) -> bool:\n   498→    label = label.strip().lower()\n   499→    return label not in {\"garbage\", \"gabrga\", \"noisy\", \"real?\"}\n   500→\n   501→\n   502→def interp_to_taxis(old_t: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_t: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   503→    old_t = np.asarray(old_t, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\n   504→    values = np.asarray(values, 523→    xpos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n   524→    ypos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n   525→\n   526→    for trix, trial 548→\n   549→def feature_speed(xpos: np.ndarray, ypos: np.ndarray, feature_name: str) -> np.ndarray:\n   550→    n_time, n_trials = xpos.shape\n   551→    xvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, np.ndarray:\n    n_time, n_trials = xpos.shape\n    xvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n    yvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        tsinterp = np.column_stack([xpos[:, i], ypos[:, i]])\n        if not np.isfinite(tsinterp).any():\n            xv = np.zeros((n_time,), dtype=np.float64)\n            yv =          else:\n                basederiv = np.array([0.0, 0.0], dtype=np.float64)\n            basederiv = np.nan_to_num(basederiv, nan=0.0)\n            xv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\n            yv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\n        if \"tongue\" not in feature_name:\n            xv = xv - basederiv[0]\n            yv = yv - basederiv[1]\n            xv = nearest_fill_1d(xv)\n No `dF/F` computation is needed.\n    51→- Neural data are sorted spike times in `obj.clu{probe}(cluster)` with per-spike session time (`tm`), per-spike within-trial time (`trialtm`), and trial index (`trial`).\n    52→- The core neural representation used downstream is `obj.trialdat`, produced by histogramming aligned spikes into fixed bins and smoothing.\n    53→- Alignment is event-based. For this task, the reference default is `goCue`, which matches the requested decoder alignment.\n    54→- Reference binning window is centered on the alignment event. Default code uses `tmin=-2.5`, `tmax=2.5`, `dt=1/200` (5 ms bins). The tutorial script in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` shows a variant with `dt=1/100` (10 ms bins), so exact choice must be cross-checked against data/text later.\n    55→- Motion energy and DLC trajectories are not used at their native frame rate directly. They are interpolated onto the same `obj.time` axis as neural activity after correcting for video offset.\n    56→- DLC/video alignment uses `frameTimes - vidshift - alignTimes(trial)`, where `vidshift` is computed from bitcode synchronization metadata.\n    57→- Motion energy loader expects one motion-energy file per ephys session and converts it to `me.data(time, trial)` on the neural time base. depend on the analysis figure and needs cross-checking later.\n    62→- Choice/context decoding scripts use balanced trial sampling across conditions and operate on `obj.trialdat`, confirming that trial-aligned binned/smoothed firing rates are the reference neural input representation.\n    63→\n    64→---\n    65→\n    66→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    67→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    68→\n    69→### Data Structure\n    70→- Top-level `data/` contains four task folders:\n    71→  - `Ephys_Behavior/`: delayed-response sessions with neural recordings and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n    72→  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/`: randomized-delay sessions with neural recordings and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n    73→  - `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only sessions (`obj.me` embedded in the session file; no `obj.clu`).\n    74→  - `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only sessions (`obj.me` embedded in the session file; no `obj.clu`).\n    75→- Session files are named `data_structure_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n    76→- Motion energy for ephys sessions is stored separately as `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n    77→- File formats are mixed:\n    78→  - Most `data_structure_*.mat` files are MATLAB v7.3 / HDF5.\n    79→  - 11 randomized-delay session files are older non-HDF5 MATLAB files and require a `scipy.io.loadmat` fallback.\n    80→- Native session object contents:\n    81→  - Ephys sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.clu`, `obj.traj`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.trials`, `obj.ex`, sometimes `obj.meta`, plus `obj.me` placeholder or struct.\n    82→  - Behavior-only sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.traj`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.trials`, `obj.ex`, embedded `obj.me`, and no `obj.clu`.\n    83→- Behavioral trial fields in `obj.bp` include `Ntrials`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `early`, `autowater`, `R`, `L`, `stim`, and event timings in `obj.bp.ev` (`bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`).\n    84→- Neural data in ephys sessions live in `obj.clu`:\n    85→  - Usually a cell array over probes.\n    86→  - Each probe contains a struct array of units with fields like `tm`, `trialtm`, `trial`, `quality`, waveform info, and channel/site.\n    87→  - Some sessions include empty probe slots, so probe handling must be robust.\n    88→- Video/DLC data live in `obj.traj` with two views per session:\n    89→  - Side view features observed in sampled sessions: `tongue`, `left_tongue`, `right_tongue`, `jaw`, `trident`, `nose`, `lickport`.\n    90→  - Bottom view features observed in sampled sessions: `top_tongue`, `topleft_tongue`, `bottom_tongue`, `bottomleft_tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`, `lickport`, `jaw`, `top_nostril`, `bottom_nostril`.\n    91→  - Each trial stores `featNames`, `ts`, `frameTimes`, and `NdroppedFrames`.\n    92→- Motion energy files/fields contain `me.data` (per-trial motion energy traces) and `me.moveThresh` (session threshold).\n    93→- Probe metadata are not perfectly uniform across sessions:\n    94→  - Many sessions expose `obj.ex.probe.loc` (example: `L ALM`, `L M1TJ`).\n    95→  - Some earlier sessions are missing that specific subfield, so brain-region mapping will need a fallback strategy.\n    96→\n    97→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n    98→| Statistic | Value |\n    99→|-----------|-------|\n   100→| Neurons (total) | 14,297 raw clustered units across ephys sessions |\n   101→| Neurons / session | 17-1,258 raw units; mean 317.71 across ephys sessions |\n   102→| Subjects | 18 total subjects in `data/`; 14 have neural recordings |\n   103→| Sessions / subject | 120 sessions total; ephys subjects contribute 1-8 sessions each |\n   104→| Trials (total) | 37,073 total trials across all session files; 15,324 in ephys sessions |\n   105→| Trials / session | 230-517 in ephys sessions; mean 340.53 (all-session mean 308.94) |\n   106→\n   107→---\n   108→\n   109→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   110→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   111→\n   112→### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n   113→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   114→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   115→| Neurons (total) | Two-context: 522 units; fixed-delay DR total: 1,651 units; randomized delay: 845 units | “two-context paradigm: 12 sessions, six mice, 522 units…”; “25 sessions, nine mice, 1,651 units…”; “randomized delay task…845 units…” |\n   116→| Neurons / session | Not given as a single mean in text; per-session stats said to be in Extended Data Fig. 2a | “see Extended Data Fig. 2a for per-session statistics” |\n   117→| Subjects | Two-context: 6 mice; fixed-delay DR total: 9 mice; randomized delay: 4 mice | “12 sessions, six mice”; “25 sessions, nine mice”; “19 sessions using four mice” |\n   118→| Sessions / subject | Two-context: 12 sessions; fixed-delay DR total: 25 sessions; randomized delay: 19 sessions | Same sentences as above |\n   119→| Trials (total) | Not reported explicitly in paper text | Not explicitly stated in methods/results |\n   120→| Trials / session | Session begins with ~100 DR trials, then 10-25 trial alternating blocks | “A behavioral session began with approximately 100 DR trials and was then followed by alternating blocks… Each interleaved block was 10–25 trials” |\n   121→| Neural data time bin | Raw extracellular traces sampled at 25 kHz; analysis bin not stated in text | “voltage traces sampled at 25 kHz and stored for offline analysis” |\n   122→| Behavior data time bin | Video acquired at 400 Hz | “High-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate) from two cameras” |\n   123→| Reward rate | Not explicitly reported as a fraction in text | Not explicitly stated in methods/results |\n   124→| Delay length (fixed DR) | 0.9 s for 12 mice; 0.7 s for one mouse, linearly warped to 0.9 s | “The delay epoch (0.9 s for 12 mice, 0.7 s for one mouse and linearly time warped to 0.9 s)” |\n   125→| Randomized delays | 0.3, 0.6, 1.2, 1.8, 2.4, 3.6 s | “randomly selected from six possible values (0.3 s, 0.6 s, 1.2 s, 1.8 s, 2.4 s and 3.6 s)” |\n   126→| Choice-selective units | 36% sample, 42% delay, 58% response of 483 single units | “choice selectivity… (sample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units)” |\n   127→| Context-selective units | 39% of 214 single units in two-context data | “39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units” |\n   128→\n   129→\n   130→### Processing Details\n   131→- Task timing from methods:\n   132→  - Sample cue lasts 1.3 s.\n   133→  - Fixed DR delay is 0.9 s for most mice; one mouse used 0.7 s and was linearly time-warped to 0.9 s.\n   134→  - Go cue is a 10 ms chirp.\n   135→  - Ignore trial if no response within 3 s of go cue.\n   136→  - In two-context sessions, all sessions begin with DR trials, then alternate WC/DR blocks of 10-25 trials.\n   137→- Alignment-relevant text:\n   138→  - The paper discusses activity “from go cue” repeatedly and the decoder figures are plotted relative to go cue / water drop, consistent with go-cue-centered alignment for DR analyses.\n   139→  - For the fixed-delay DR task, preparatory dynamics are discussed across sample and delay epochs leading up to the go cue.\n   140→- Video/kinematics processing from text:\n   141→  - Two 400 Hz cameras: side and bottom.\n   142→  - Tongue, jaw and nose tracked in both views; paws tracked in bottom view only.\n   143→  - Missing position values filled with nearest values for all features except tongue.\n   144→  - Velocity is the first derivative of position.\n   145→  - Tongue angle and length are derived from bottom view.\n   146→- Motion-energy processing from text:\n   147→  - Frame-wise motion energy is based on the absolute difference between the median of the next 5 frames and previous 5 frames.\n   148→  - Per-frame motion energy summary is the 99th percentile across pixels.\n   149→  - Session-specific movement threshold is chosen manually from a bimodal motion-energy distribution.\n   150→- Electrophysiology processing from text:\n   151→  - Spikes were sorted with JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3, manually curated in Phy 2.\n   152→  - Recordings were made in ALM.\n   153→  - Sessions included only if they had at least 10 units.\n   154→  - For subspace alignment and single-unit selectivity analyses: use well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   155→  - For all other analyses: use all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   156→\n   157→### Curation Steps\n   158→\n   159→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   160→- Well-isolated single units are defined by manual curation using ISI histogram, separation from other units, and stationarity across session.\n   161→- Multiunits are manually curated units with higher ISI violation rates.\n   162→- Recording sessions included only if they contained at least 10 units.\n   163→- Analysis-specific neuron filter:\n   164→  - Subspace alignment and single-unit selectivity: well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   165→  - Other analyses: all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n   166→\n   167→**Trial curation rules**:\n   168→- Early licks are omitted from analyses.\n   169→- Ignore trials are omitted from analyses.\n   170→- Behavioral-session inclusion for behavior analyses: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction, excluding early lick and ignore trials.\n   171→- Randomized delay animals trained to expert criterion >70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate.\n   172→\n   173→### Decoders Trained\n   174→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   175→| Upcoming choice from `CDchoice` projections | AUC = 0.86 ± 0.11 across sessions |\n   176→| Choice from uninstructed movements (Fig. 3b / 3g) | Above chance and increases through delay; exact scalar accuracy not stated in text |\n   177→| Context from kinematic features and neural population (Fig. 4b) | Above shuffled across epochs including ITI; exact scalar accuracy not stated in text |\n   178→| Single-trial `CDchoice` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.41 ± 0.23` across sessions |\n   179→| Single-trial `CDramp` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.46 ± 0.23` across sessions |\n   180→| Single-trial `CDcontext` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.49 ± 0.22` across sessions |\n   181→\n   182→---\n   183→\n   184→## Use ALM as the brain-region label for neurons on that selected probe. |\n   194→| MATLAB file format | MATLAB code can transparently load both v7.3 and older `.mat` files | `data/` mixes HDF5/v7.3 and older MATLAB files | Paper does not mention file-format heterogeneity | Python conversion must support dual loading: `h5py` for v7.3/HDF5 session files and `scipy.io.loadmat` fallback for older `.mat` files. |\n   195→| Neural bin width | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` example uses `dt = 1/100` (10 ms); `getDefaultParams.m` uses `dt = 1/200` (5 ms) | Raw spikes are timestamped continuously; no native analysis bin | Methods text gives 25 kHz acquisition but not the final analysis bin | Treat `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` as illustrative. Use the default analysis pipeline setting only after verifying against later processing needs; tentatively prefer the paper/code-aligned 5 ms default, but keep this as a validation target in later steps. |\n   196→| Low firing-rate threshold | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` example uses `lowFR = 1`; `getDefaultParams.m` uses `lowFR = 0.5`; Fig. 8 script uses `lowFR = 1` | Raw data contain many low-count clusters | Methods state units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in analyses | Resolve in favor of the methods text and figure scripts: use a 1 Hz low-FR threshold when matching the analyzed neural population, then verify counts against the paper. |\n   197→| Cluster quality usage | Default code commonly sets `params.quality = {'all'}` and `findClusters` excludes only obvious bad labels | Raw data include manual quality strings and mixed unit types | Methods distinguish well-isolated single units versus multiunits; “all units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz” were used for most analyses | For the decoder conversion, use all non-garbage/non-noisy manually curated units on the selected ALM probe, then apply the FR threshold. Reserve single-unit-only filtering only for analyses where the paper explicitly did so. |\n   198→| Context alignment event | Figure text sometimes describes alignment to “go cue / water drop”; code default alignment is `goCue` | `obj.bp.ev.goCue` exists for all inspected sessions, including autowater/WC trials | User explicitly requires alignment to go cue onset | Use the stored `bp.ev.goCue` field for all trials as the universal alignment event in the converted dataset. |\n   199→\n   200→---\n   201→\n   202→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   203→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   204→\n   205→### Variable Mapping\n   206→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   207→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   208→| Code-selected ALM probe from `load*_ALMVideo.m` + `obj.clu{probe}` spike times | `neural` | Keep units from the session’s ALM-designated probe only; exclude bad-quality labels via `findClusters`; align spikes to `bp.ev.goCue`; bin on a fixed global time grid; smooth as in reference; drop units with mean FR `<= 1 Hz`; output one `(n_neurons, n_timepoints)` matrix per trial | `load*_ALMVideo.m`, `findClusters`, `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `removeLowFRClusters` | Use only the 44 sessions in the intersection of reference loader lists and available data files |\n   209→| `obj.time` / aligned bin centers relative to `goCue` | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue vector repeated for every trial: shape `(1, n_timepoints)` | `getSeq` | Decoder input requested by user |\n   210→| `bp.R`, `bp.L` | `output[0]` | Lick direction: left `0`, right `1`; encode as a constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials` conditions use `R` and `L` throughout | Keep only non-early hit/miss trials so direction is well defined |\n   211→| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Behavioral context: WC `0`, DR `1` via `1 - autowater`; constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials`; context conditions in Fig. 8 scripts | Stored `autowater=1` corresponds to WC in the reference code/paper |\n   212→| `bp.hit`, `bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Outcome: miss `0`, hit `1`; constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials` | Exclude `no` and `early` trials rather than inventing a label for ignores |\n   213→| Reference-aligned kinematics from side-camera tongue marker velocities | `output[3]` | Compute tongue speed as `sqrt(tongue_xvel_view1^2 + tongue_yvel_view1^2)` after reference interpolation/fill rules; binarize by session median over all kept samples | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematicsFromVideo` | Uses the canonical `tongue` marker from the side view to avoid mixing camera coordinate systems |\n   214→| Reference-aligned kinematics from bottom-camera paw markers | `output[4]` | Compute top- and bottom-paw speed magnitudes from x/y velocity pairs, average them per timepoint, then binarize by session median over all kept samples | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematicsFromVideo` | Paws are tracked in bottom view only per methods |\n   215→| Aligned motion-energy trace `me.data` | `output[5]` | Use aligned continuous motion energy and binarize by session median over all kept samples | `loadMotionEnergy` | The paper uses a manual movement threshold for move/non-move analyses; the user explicitly requests a 50th-percentile discretization for decoder output |\n   216→| Animal IDs from filenames / loader scripts | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list across retained sessions | `load*_ALMVideo.m` | Expected 14 unique neural subjects in the available dataset |\n   217→| Selected probe region | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Label neurons from the code-selected probe as `ALM` | `load*_ALMVideo.m` | Raw metadata can include non-ALM probes, but the loader scripts resolve which probe to use |\n   218→\n   219→### Key Decisions\n   220→1. **Retain only the 44 code-selected ALM sessions present in `data/`**: This is the cleanest way to match the paper’s analyzed dataset rather than the broader raw archive.\n   221→2. **Use the ALM-designated probe only for each retained session**: Raw files can contain multiple probes/areas; the reference loaders specify which probe corresponds to ALM.\n   222→3. **Exclude stimulation, early-lick, and ignore/no-response trials**: Reference analyses consistently use `~stim.enable`, `~early`, and usually hit/miss conditions; ignore trials are omitted in the paper.\n   223→4. **Use `goCue` as the universal alignment event**: This matches the user request and the default reference alignment.\n   224→5. **Use a fixed window of `[-2.5, 2.5] s` around `goCue` with a 5 ms bin (`dt = 1/200`)**: This matches the default reference processing pipeline better than the 10 ms tutorial example and gives a shared time axis for neural/video streams.\n   225→6. **Use all non-garbage manually curated units on the selected probe, then apply a 1 Hz FR threshold**: This matches the paper’s “all units >1 Hz” rule for most analyses better than a single-unit-only subset.\n   226→7. **Represent trial-level categorical outputs as constant time series**: This keeps all outputs on the same `(n_output, n_timepoints)` grid while preserving the per-trial labels requested by the user.\n   227→8. **Define tongue velocity from the side-view `tongue` marker speed magnitude**: This uses a direct reference-processed kinematic channel without inventing an unreferenced cross-camera combination.\n   228→9. **Define paw velocity from the average of top- and bottom-paw speed magnitudes in the bottom view**: This captures overall paw movement while remaining close to the tracked features described in the paper/code.\n   229→10. **Use per-session 50th-percentile thresholds for tongue velocity, paw velocity, and motion energy**: This follows the decoder task even where it differs from the paper’s manual movement threshold.\n   230→\n   231→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   232→- [ ] Verify that retained session list exactly matches the 44-session intersection of reference loader scripts and available data files.\n   233→- [ ] Compare retained fixed-delay and randomized-delay session counts against the paper’s 25 and 19 session totals.\n   234→- [ ] After probe selection and 1 Hz filtering, compare unit counts against the paper’s reported 1,651 fixed-delay and 845 randomized-delay units.\n   235→- [ ] Spot-check one neuron in one trial by histogramming raw spike times directly from the source file and comparing to the converted binned/smoothed trace.\n   236→- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s `lick_direction`, `context`, and `outcome` labels directly from raw `bp` fields using `np.allclose()`.\n   237→- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s aligned motion energy against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using the source frame times / video offset logic.\n   238→- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s aligned tongue and paw velocity traces against direct interpolation of raw DLC coordinates followed by the same velocity computation.\n   239→- [ ] Check that all trials in the converted dataset share the same time vector and that `input[0]` matches the neural bin centers exactly.\n   240→\n   241→---\n   242→\n   243→## Step 6: Script Development\n   244→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   245→\n   246→[Implementation notes]\n   247→- Created `convert_data.py`.\n   248→- Script design:\n   249→  - Parses the reference ALM session loader scripts to recover the analyzed session list and probe selections.\n   250→  - Supports both MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 session files (`h5py`) and older MATLAB session files (`scipy.io.loadmat`).\n   251→  - Reimplements the reference spike binning and smoothing path in Python, including the causal Gaussian kernel behavior in `mySmooth.m`.\n   252→  - Loads and aligns motion energy and selected DLC features to the neural time base using the reference video-offset logic.\n   253→  - Filters to non-stim, non-early hit/miss trials and builds decoder-ready `input`/`output` arrays.\n   254→  - Supports `--full`, `--sample`, and `--show-processing`.\n   255→\n   256→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   257→- Full recursive HDF5-to-Python loading was too slow and memory-heavy for the large session files.\n   258→- Per-spike/per-trial histogram loops would likely be a bottleneck if implemented naively.\n   259→\n   260→Code speedups added:\n   261→- Implemented targeted field loading instead of full-session recursive decoding for HDF5 files.\n   262→- Implemented vectorized per-unit spike accumulation with `np.add.at` instead of calling a histogram inside nested trial loops.\n   263→- Processes one session at a time to keep memory bounded.\n   264→\n   265→---\n   266→\n   267→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n Dataset Conversion Notes\n\n## Overview\n- **Dataset**: Separating cognitive and motor processes in the behaving mouse\n- **Date started**: 2026-03-21\n- **Goal**: Convert to decoder-compatible format\n\n## Step 0: Setup and Initialization\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nDirectory contents:\n- `.manifest`\n- `CONVERSION_NOTES.md`\n- `Dockerfile`\n- `code/`\n- `data/`\n- `decoder.py`\n- `docker-compose.yaml`\n- `methods.txt`\n- `paper.pdf`\n- `train_decoder.py`\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Reference Code Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `loadObjs` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadObjs.m` | LOADING | Loads each session `.mat` into `obj`; ensures missing fields (`meta`, `ex`, `me`) exist so structs concatenate cleanly. |\n| `loadSessionData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadSessionData.m` | LOADING | Main session loader; calls `loadObjs`, then `processData` for each requested probe, and concatenates probes within session. |\n| `processData` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/processData.m` | PROCESSING | Reference pipeline driver: finds trials, finds clusters, aligns spikes, bins/smooths spikes, removes low-FR units, computes presample baseline statistics. |\n| `findTrials` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findTrials.m` | CURATION | Evaluates logical trial-condition expressions against `obj.bp.*` fields and returns matching trial indices. |\n| `findClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/findClusters.m` | CURATION | Selects clusters by quality; special case `all` keeps all except `garbage`, `gabrga`, `noisy`, `real?`. |\n| `alignSpikes` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/alignSpikes.m` | PROCESSING | Aligns per-spike trial times to chosen event (`goCue`, `moveOnset`, `firstLick`, `lastLick`, `jawOnset`). |\n| `getSeq` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getSeq.m` | PROCESSING | Bins aligned spikes on `edges = tmin:dt:tmax`, smooths them, creates trial-averaged PSTHs and single-trial neural matrices. |\n| `removeLowFRClusters` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/removeLowFRClusters.m` | CURATION | Drops units with mean firing rate below threshold based on PSTHs across conditions. |\n| `baselineFR` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/baselineFR.m` | PROCESSING | Computes presample baseline firing rate and sigma from unaligned spike times. |\n| `getDefaultParams` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/getDefaultParams.m` | PROCESSING | Defines default alignment (`goCue`), time window (`-2.5` to `2.5` s), neural bin width (`dt = 1/200`), low-FR threshold (`0.5` Hz), and DLC features. |\n| `loadMotionEnergy` | `code/DataLoadingScripts/loadMotionEnergy.m` | LOADING | Loads per-trial motion energy, aligns to chosen event using video offset, interpolates onto `obj.time`, fills missing samples, thresholds movement. |\n| `findVideoOffset` | `code/funcs/findVideoOffset.m` | PROCESSING | Computes video-to-neural timing offset from bitcode metadata (`sglx.bitcode.bitstart/fs - bp.ev.bitStart`). |\n| `findPosition` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findPosition.m` | PROCESSING | Extracts DLC x/y trajectories for one body part, applies video offset, aligns to event, interpolates onto neural time axis. |\n| `findVelocity` | `code/funcs/kinematics/findVelocity.m` | PROCESSING | Computes per-trial x/y velocity from interpolated positions; tongue NaNs become zero velocity, non-tongue NaNs filled by nearest. |\n| `getKinematicsFromVideo` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematicsFromVideo.m` | PROCESSING | Builds full kinematics tensor from DLC positions and velocities for selected features across views. |\n| `getKinematics` | `code/funcs/kinematics/getKinematics.m` | PROCESSING | Adds tongue angle/length and motion energy to kinematic features, standardizes them, and performs dimensionality reduction. |\n| `UseInclusionCritera` | `code/utils/UseInclusionCritera.m` | CURATION | Drops sessions with fewer than 40 right-hit and 40 left-hit trials. |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference dataset is electrophysiology plus behavior/video, not calcium imaging. No `dF/F` computation is needed.\n- Neural data are sorted spike times in `obj.clu{probe}(cluster)` with per-spike session time (`tm`), per-spike within-trial time (`trialtm`), and trial index (`trial`).\n- The core neural representation used downstream is `obj.trialdat`, produced by histogramming aligned spikes into fixed bins and smoothing.\n- Alignment is event-based. For this task, the reference default is `goCue`, which matches the requested decoder alignment.\n- Reference binning window is centered on the alignment event. Default code uses `tmin=-2.5`, `tmax=2.5`, `dt=1/200` (5 ms bins). The tutorial script in `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` shows a variant with `dt=1/100` (10 ms bins), so exact choice must be cross-checked against data/text later.\n- Motion energy and DLC trajectories are not used at their native frame rate directly. They are interpolated onto the same `obj.time` axis as neural activity after correcting for video offset.\n- DLC/video alignment uses `frameTimes - vidshift - alignTimes(trial)`, where `vidshift` is computed from bitcode synchronization metadata.\n- Motion energy loader expects one motion-energy file per ephys session and converts it to `me.data(time, trial)` on the neural time base. It also defines a binary `me.move` using a stored session threshold.\n- Cluster curation has two stages in code:\n  1. Quality filter by string label via `findClusters`.\n  2. Low-firing-rate removal via `removeLowFRClusters`.\n- Session-level inclusion criterion found in code: at least 40 right-hit and 40 left-hit trials (`UseInclusionCritera`), though this may depend on the analysis figure and needs cross-checking later.\n- Choice/context decoding scripts use balanced trial sampling across conditions and operate on `obj.trialdat`, confirming that trial-aligned binned/smoothed firing rates are the reference neural input representation.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- Top-level `data/` contains four task folders:\n  - `Ephys_Behavior/`: delayed-response sessions with neural recordings and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `RandomizedDelay_Ephys_Behavior/`: randomized-delay sessions with neural recordings and separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files.\n  - `DelayInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only sessions (`obj.me` embedded in the session file; no `obj.clu`).\n  - `GoCueInhibition_BilatMC_Behavior/`: behavior-only sessions (`obj.me` embedded in the session file; no `obj.clu`).\n- Session files are named `data_structure_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n- Motion energy for ephys sessions is stored separately as `motionEnergy_<animal>_<date>.mat`.\n- File formats are mixed:\n  - Most `data_structure_*.mat` files are MATLAB v7.3 / HDF5.\n  - 11 randomized-delay session files are older non-HDF5 MATLAB files and require a `scipy.io.loadmat` fallback.\n- Native session object contents:\n  - Ephys sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.clu`, `obj.traj`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.trials`, `obj.ex`, sometimes `obj.meta`, plus `obj.me` placeholder or struct.\n  - Behavior-only sessions: `obj.bp`, `obj.traj`, `obj.sglx`, `obj.trials`, `obj.ex`, embedded `obj.me`, and no `obj.clu`.\n- Behavioral trial fields in `obj.bp` include `Ntrials`, `hit`, `miss`, `no`, `early`, `autowater`, `R`, `L`, `stim`, and event timings in `obj.bp.ev` (`bitStart`, `sample`, `delay`, `goCue`, `reward`, `lickL`, `lickR`).\n- Neural data in ephys sessions live in `obj.clu`:\n  - Usually a cell array over probes.\n  - Each probe contains a struct array of units with fields like `tm`, `trialtm`, `trial`, `quality`, waveform info, and channel/site.\n  - Some sessions include empty probe slots, so probe handling must be robust.\n- Video/DLC data live in `obj.traj` with two views per session:\n  - Side view features observed in sampled sessions: `tongue`, `left_tongue`, `right_tongue`, `jaw`, `trident`, `nose`, `lickport`.\n  - Bottom view features observed in sampled sessions: `top_tongue`, `topleft_tongue`, `bottom_tongue`, `bottomleft_tongue`, `top_paw`, `bottom_paw`, `lickport`, `jaw`, `top_nostril`, `bottom_nostril`.\n  - Each trial stores `featNames`, `ts`, `frameTimes`, and `NdroppedFrames`.\n- Motion energy files/fields contain `me.data` (per-trial motion energy traces) and `me.moveThresh` (session threshold).\n- Probe metadata are not perfectly uniform across sessions:\n  - Many sessions expose `obj.ex.probe.loc` (example: `L ALM`, `L M1TJ`).\n  - Some earlier sessions are missing that specific subfield, so brain-region mapping will need a fallback strategy.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (total) | 14,297 raw clustered units across ephys sessions |\n| Neurons / session | 17-1,258 raw units; mean 317.71 across ephys sessions |\n| Subjects | 18 total subjects in `data/`; 14 have neural recordings |\n| Sessions / subject | 120 sessions total; ephys subjects contribute 1-8 sessions each |\n| Trials (total) | 37,073 total trials across all session files; 15,324 in ephys sessions |\n| Trials / session | 230-517 in ephys sessions; mean 340.53 (all-session mean 308.94) timing from methods:\n  - Sample cue lasts 1.3 s.\n  - Fixed DR delay is 0.9 s for most mice; one mouse used 0.7 s and was linearly time-warped to 0.9 s.\n  - Go cue is a 10 ms chirp.\n  - Ignore trial if no response within 3 s of go cue.\n  - In two-context sessions, all sessions begin with DR trials, then alternate WC/DR blocks of 10-25 trials.\n- Alignment-relevant text:\n  - The paper discusses activity “from go cue” repeatedly and the decoder figures are plotted relative to go cue / water drop, consistent with go-cue-centered alignment for DR analyses.\n  - For the fixed-delay DR task, preparatory dynamics are discussed across sample and delay epochs leading up to the go cue.\n- Video/kinematics processing from text:\n  - Two 400 Hz cameras: side and bottom.\n  - Tongue, jaw and nose tracked in both views; paws tracked in bottom view only.\n  - Missing position values filled with nearest values for all features except tongue.\n  - Velocity is the first derivative of position.\n  - Tongue angle and length are derived from bottom view.\n- Motion-energy processing from text:\n  - Frame-wise motion energy is based on the absolute difference between the median of the next 5 frames and previous 5 frames.\n  - Per-frame motion energy summary is the 99th percentile across pixels.\n  - Session-specific movement threshold is chosen manually from a bimodal motion-energy distribution.\n- Electrophysiology processing from text:\n  - Spikes were sorted with JRCLUST and/or Kilosort 3, manually curated in Phy 2.\n  - Recordings were made in ALM.\n  - Sessions included only if they had at least 10 units.\n  - For subspace alignment and single-unit selectivity analyses: use well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n  - For all other analyses: use all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Well-isolated single units are defined by manual curation using ISI histogram, separation from other units, and stationarity across session.\n- Multiunits are manually curated units with higher ISI violation rates.\n- Recording sessions included only if they contained at least 10 units.\n- Analysis-specific neuron filter:\n  - Subspace alignment and single-unit selectivity: well-isolated single units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n  - Other analyses: all units with firing rates >1 Hz.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Early licks are omitted from analyses.\n- Ignore trials are omitted from analyses.\n- Behavioral-session inclusion for behavior analyses: at least 40 correct DR trials per direction and 20 correct WC trials per direction, excluding early lick and ignore trials.\n- Randomized delay animals trained to expert criterion >70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Upcoming choice from `CDchoice` projections | AUC = 0.86 ± 0.11 across sessions |\n| Choice from uninstructed movements (Fig. 3b / 3g) | Above chance and increases through delay; exact scalar accuracy not stated in text |\n| Context from kinematic features and neural population (Fig. 4b) | Above shuffled across epochs including ITI; exact scalar accuracy not stated in text |\n| Single-trial `CDchoice` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.41 ± 0.23` across sessions |\n| Single-trial `CDramp` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.46 ± 0.23` across sessions |\n| Single-trial `CDcontext` prediction from kinematics | `R2 = 0.49 ± 0.22` across sessions |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Use the default analysis pipeline setting only after verifying against later processing needs; tentatively prefer the paper/code-aligned 5 ms default, but keep this as a validation target in later steps. |\n| Low firing-rate threshold | `WorkingWithDataObjs.m` example uses `lowFR = 1`; `getDefaultParams.m` uses `lowFR = 0.5`; Fig. 8 script uses `lowFR = 1` | Raw data contain many low-count clusters | Methods state units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz were included in analyses | Resolve in favor of the methods text and figure scripts: use a 1 Hz low-FR threshold when matching the analyzed neural population, then verify counts against the paper. |\n| Cluster quality usage | Default code commonly sets `params.quality = {'all'}` and `findClusters` excludes only obvious bad labels | Raw data include manual quality strings and mixed unit types | Methods distinguish well-isolated single units versus multiunits; “all units with firing rates exceeding 1 Hz” were used for most analyses | For the decoder conversion, use all non-garbage/non-noisy manually curated units on the selected ALM probe, then apply the FR threshold. Reserve single-unit-only filtering only for analyses where the paper explicitly did so. |\n| Context alignment event | Figure text sometimes describes alignment to “go cue / water drop”; code default alignment is `goCue` | `obj.bp.ev.goCue` exists for all inspected sessions, including autowater/WC trials | User explicitly requires alignment to go cue onset | Use the stored `bp.ev.goCue` field for all trials as the universal alignment event in the converted dataset. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| Code-selected ALM probe from `load*_ALMVideo.m` + `obj.clu{probe}` spike times | `neural` | Keep units from the session’s ALM-designated probe only; exclude bad-quality labels via `findClusters`; align spikes to `bp.ev.goCue`; bin on a fixed global time grid; smooth as in reference; drop units with mean FR `<= 1 Hz`; output one `(n_neurons, n_timepoints)` matrix per trial | `load*_ALMVideo.m`, `findClusters`, `alignSpikes`, `getSeq`, `removeLowFRClusters` | Use only the 44 sessions in the intersection of reference loader lists and available data files |\n| `obj.time` / aligned bin centers relative to `goCue` | `input[0]` | Continuous time-from-go-cue vector repeated for every trial: shape `(1, n_timepoints)` | `getSeq` | Decoder input requested by user |\n| `bp.R`, `bp.L` | `output[0]` | Lick direction: left `0`, right `1`; encode as a constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials` conditions use `R` and `L` throughout | Keep only non-early hit/miss trials so direction is well defined |\n| `bp.autowater` | `output[1]` | Behavioral context: WC `0`, DR `1` via `1 - autowater`; constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials`; context conditions in Fig. 8 scripts | Stored `autowater=1` corresponds to WC in the reference code/paper |\n| `bp.hit`, `bp.miss` | `output[2]` | Outcome: miss `0`, hit `1`; constant time series over the trial window | `findTrials` | Exclude `no` and `early` trials rather than inventing a label for ignores |\n| Reference-aligned kinematics from side-camera tongue marker velocities | `output[3]` | Compute tongue speed as `sqrt(tongue_xvel_view1^2 + tongue_yvel_view1^2)` after reference interpolation/fill rules; binarize by session median over all kept samples | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematicsFromVideo` | Uses the canonical `tongue` marker from the side view to avoid mixing camera coordinate systems |\n| Reference-aligned kinematics from bottom-camera paw markers | `output[4]` | Compute top- and bottom-paw speed magnitudes from x/y velocity pairs, average them per timepoint, then binarize by session median over all kept samples | `findPosition`, `findVelocity`, `getKinematicsFromVideo` | Paws are tracked in bottom view only per methods |\n| Aligned motion-energy trace `me.data` | `output[5]` | Use aligned continuous motion energy and binarize by session median over all kept samples | `loadMotionEnergy` | The paper uses a manual movement threshold for move/non-move analyses; the user explicitly requests a 50th-percentile discretization for decoder output |\n| Animal IDs from filenames / loader scripts | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Build unique subject list across retained sessions | `load*_ALMVideo.m` | Expected 14 unique neural subjects in the available dataset |\n| Selected probe region | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | Label neurons from the code-selected probe as `ALM` | `load*_ALMVideo.m` | Raw metadata can include non-ALM probes, but the loader scripts resolve which probe to use |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Retain only the 44 code-selected ALM sessions present in `data/`**: This is the cleanest way to match the paper’s analyzed dataset rather than the broader raw archive.\n2. **Use the ALM-designated probe only for each retained session**: Raw files can contain multiple probes/areas; the reference loaders specify which probe corresponds to ALM.\n3. **Exclude stimulation, early-lick, and ignore/no-response trials**: Reference analyses consistently use `~stim.enable`, `~early`, and usually hit/miss conditions; ignore trials are omitted in the paper.\n4. **Use `goCue` as the universal alignment event**: This matches the user request and the default reference alignment.\n5. **Use a fixed window of `[-2.5, 2.5] s` around `goCue` with a 5 ms bin (`dt = 1/200`)**: This matches the default reference processing pipeline better than the 10 ms tutorial example and gives a shared time axis for neural/video streams.\n6. **Use all non-garbage manually curated units on the selected probe, then apply a 1 Hz FR threshold**: This matches the paper’s “all units >1 Hz” rule for most analyses better than a single-unit-only subset.\n7. **Represent trial-level categorical outputs as constant time series**: This keeps all outputs on the same `(n_output, n_timepoints)` grid while preserving the per-trial labels requested by the user.\n8. **Define tongue velocity from the side-view `tongue` marker speed magnitude**: This uses a direct reference-processed kinematic channel without inventing an unreferenced cross-camera combination.\n9. **Define paw velocity from the average of top- and bottom-paw speed magnitudes in the bottom view**: This captures overall paw movement while remaining close to the tracked features described in the paper/code.\n10. **Use per-session 50th-percentile thresholds for tongue velocity, paw velocity, and motion energy**: This follows the decoder task even where it differs from the paper’s manual movement threshold.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Verify that retained session list exactly matches the 44-session intersection of reference loader scripts and available data files.\n- [ ] Compare retained fixed-delay and randomized-delay session counts against the paper’s 25 and 19 session totals.\n- [ ] After probe selection and 1 Hz filtering, compare unit counts against the paper’s reported 1,651 fixed-delay and 845 randomized-delay units.\n- [ ] Spot-check one neuron in one trial by histogramming raw spike times directly from the source file and comparing to the converted binned/smoothed trace.\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s `lick_direction`, `context`, and `outcome` labels directly from raw `bp` fields using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s aligned motion energy against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using the source frame times / video offset logic.\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial’s aligned tongue and paw velocity traces against direct interpolation of raw DLC coordinates followed by the same velocity computation.\n- [ ] Check that all trials in the converted dataset share the same time vector and that `input[0]` matches the neural bin centers exactly.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n[Implementation notes]\n- Created `convert_data.py`.\n- Script design:\n  - Parses the reference ALM session loader scripts to recover the analyzed session list and probe selections.\n  - Supports both MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 session files (`h5py`) and older MATLAB session files (`scipy.io.loadmat`).\n  - Reimplements the reference spike binning and smoothing path in Python, including the causal Gaussian kernel behavior in `mySmooth.m`.\n  - Loads and aligns motion energy and selected DLC features to the neural time base using the reference video-offset logic.\n  - Filters to non-stim, non-early hit/miss trials and builds decoder-ready `input`/`output` arrays.\n  - Supports `--full`, `--sample`, and `--show-processing`.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Full recursive HDF5-to-Python loading was too slow and memory-heavy for the large session files.\n- Per-spike/per-trial histogram loops would likely be a bottleneck if implemented naively.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Implemented targeted field loading instead of full-session recursive decoding for HDF5 files.\n- Implemented vectorized per-unit spike accumulation with `np.add.at` instead of calling a histogram inside nested trial loops.\n- Processes one session at a time to keep memory bounded.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion From CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 5: \"Define tongue velocity from the side-view tongue marker speed magnitude.\"\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue velocity*?\n\ni. (1) DLC x/y positions are **interpolated** onto the neural time axis using `np.interp`. (2) Velocity is computed as the magnitude of `np.gradient` of x and y. (3) For tongue specifically, NaN velocity values are replaced with 0 (not nearest-filled). (4) Timepoints where both x and y positions are NaN are marked as invalid. (5) The continuous speed is thresholded at the session 50th percentile.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef feature_xy(raw, view_idx, feature_name, align_times, time_vec):\n    # interpolates x, y onto time_vec\n    xpos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 0], taxis)\n    ypos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 1], taxis)\n\ndef feature_speed(xpos, ypos, feature_name):\n    xv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\n    yv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\n    # For tongue: nan_to_num(xv, nan=0.0)\n    return np.sqrt(xvel**2 + yvel**2)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Paw velocity*?\n\ni. Same as tongue: (1) interpolate x/y positions to neural time axis, (2) compute speed as magnitude of gradient. The two paw speeds are averaged where both are available. NaN paw speeds are filled with 0.0 before thresholding.\n\nii.\n```python\n# In feature_speed, for non-tongue:\nbasederiv = np.nanmedian(deriv, axis=0)\nxv = xv - basederiv[0]\nyv = yv - basederiv[1]\nxv = nearest_fill_1d(xv)\nyv = nearest_fill_1d(yv)\n\n# Averaging two paws:\npaw_speed = np.divide(paw_sum, np.maximum(paw_count, 1), ...)\npaw_speed = np.nan_to_num(paw_speed, nan=0.0)\n```\n\niii. The baseline subtraction and nearest fill match the reference `findVelocity.m` behavior for non-tongue features.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Paw velocity* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. This follows the reference `loadMotionEnergy.m` interpolation approach.\n\n## 10. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several strategies: (1) Missing frame times: if `frame_times` is None or all NaN, a synthetic time axis is created assuming 400 Hz (`(np.arange(ts.shape[0]) + 1.0) / 400.0`). (2) For tongue, NaN speeds are set to 0 and assigned class 0. (3) For non-tongue features, NaN positions and velocities are nearest-filled. (4) Motion energy NaNs are nearest-filled then zero-filled. (5) Sessions with fewer than 10 units or fewer than 2 valid trials are skipped.
